# Powers conferred by the proviso to article 309 and clause (5) of article 148 of the Constitution, and after consultation with the Comptroller and Auditor-General in relation to persons serving in the Indian Audit and Accounts Department, the President is pleased to make the rules.

MINISTRY OF FINANCE (Department of Expenditure) NOTIFICATION New Delhi, the 2nd August 1980 S.O. 1962.—In exercise of the powers conferred by the proviso to article 309 and clause (5) of article 148 of the Constitution, and after consultation with the Comptroller and Auditor-General in relation to persons serving in the Indian Audit and Accounts Department, the President is pleased to make the following rules, namely:—

1. Short title and commencement.—(i) These rules may be called the Central Civil Services (Revised Pay) Rules, 1960.

(ii) They shall be deemed to have come into force on the 1st day of July, 1959.

2. Categories of Government servants to whom the rules apply.—(i) Save as otherwise provided by or under these rules, these rules shall apply to persons appointed to civil services and posts in connection with the affairs of the Union including persons serving in the Indian Audit and Accounts Department and whose pay is debitable to the Civil Estimates.

(ii) These rules shall not apply to:—

(a) persons who having been appointed by the Secretary of State or the Secretary of State-in-Council to a civil service of the Crown in India continue on and after the commencement of the Constitution of India to serve under the Government of India;

(b) former King's Commissioned Indian Officers in civil employ;

(c) members of the Indian Foreign Service and the Indian Frontier Administrative Service;

(d) permanent employees of former Indian States absorbed in civil services and posts in connection with the affairs of the Union so long as such persons continue to be governed by the pre-absorption conditions of service under the Central Civil Services (Part B States Transferred Employees) Rules, 1953;

(e) persons locally recruited for service in Diplomatic, Consular or other Indian Establishments in foreign countries;

(f) persons not in whole time employment;

(g) persons paid out of contingencies;

(h) persons paid otherwise than on a monthly basis including those paid only on a piece-rate basis;

(i) persons employed on contract except when the contract provides otherwise;

[ P \ R T 1 !

(j) re-employed Government servants;

(k) persons specifically excluded wholly or in part by the PresiHent from the operation of these rules.

3. Relaxation of Rules.—Where the President is satisfied that the operation ol! any of these rules causes undue hardship in any particular case, he may, by order dispense with or relax the requirements of that rule to such extent and subject to such conditions as he may consider necessary for dealing with the case in a just and equitable manner.

4. Power of Interpretation.—If any question arises relating to the interpretation of these rules it shall be roferied to the Central Government whose decision thereon shall be final.

5. Definitions.—In these rules, unless there is anything repugnant* in the .subject or context:

(1; 'basic pay' means pay as defined in Fundamental Rule 9(21)(a)(i), provided that where special pay forms a part of the existing scale and is merged in the revised scale, basic pay includes special pay.

(2) 'emoluments' means basic pay, and where admissible, also dearne^t, pay and dearness allowance;

(3) 'existing scale' means the scale of pay applicable to a Government servant in respect of the post held by him in a substantive or officiating capacity on the 1st day of July, 1959.

Explanation.—In the rase of a Government servant on deputation out of India or on leave or on foreign service, or one who would have officiated in one or more lower posts but for his officiating in a higher post, 'existing scale' includes the scale applicable to the post which be would have held but for his being on deputation out of India or on leave or on foreign service or but for officiating in another post.

(4) 'post-1931 entrant' means a Government servant other than a prc-1931 entrant;

(5) 'pro-1931 entrant' means a Government servant in continuous employ of the Government of India from the 16th August, 1947 or any earlier date, and who either was continuously in Government service in a substantive or other capacity from a date prior to the 16th July, lr'31 and was not governed by the Revised Rates of Pay Rules, 1933;

OR entered Government service on or after the 16th July, 1931 but was wholly exempted from the operation of the Revised Rates of Pay Rules, 1933, by special orders;

Explanation 1.—Service of a Government servant re-employed after retirement shall not be deemed to be continuous for the purpose of this clause. On ICemployment he shall be treated as a post-1931 entrant.

Explanation, 2—In this clause 'Government service' means service under the Government of India and includes, as respects any period before the 16th August, 1947, service under a Provincial Government or a local fund administered by Government.

(6) 'pre-1931 scale' means the scale of pay specified in column 2 of the schedule.

(7) 'prescribed scale' means the scale of pay specified in column 3 of the schedule.

(8) 'present emoluments' means the basic pay of a Government servant in the existing scale, and dearness allowance including dearness pay (at the married rates in all cases where two separate rates exist for married and unmarried officers) appropriate to the basic pay on the 1st day of July, 1959.

(9) "revised emoluments' means the basic pay of a Government servant in the revised scale and the revised dearness allowance, if any appropriate to the basic pay;

THE GA/FT1E OF INDIA EVi RAO R DINAR* 439/3

(10) 'revised scaic' means the scale of pay specified in column 4 of the schedule.

(11) 'schedule' means the schedule annexed to these rules.

6. Scale of pay of posts.—The scales of pay of posts specified in the schedule shall be those specified therein.

7. Drawal of pay in revised scales.—Save as otherwise provided in these rules, a Government servant shall draw pay in the revised scale applicable to the post to which he is appointed:

Provided that a pre-1931 entrant who does not or ig not entitled to elect the revised scale under rule 8(a), shall, when appointed to a post which carries a pre-1931 scale of pay, draw pay in that scale.

8. Option to elect revised scales,—The revised scales of pay shown in the schedule shall apply to-'—

(a) pre-1931 entrants who have not elected the prescribed scales applicable to them but who elect the revised scales of pay, provided that such option shall be admissible only if the prescribed scales of all the posts held by a Government servant in a substantive as well as officiating capacity have undergone a revision under these rules; and

(b) pre-1931 entrants who had elected the prescribed scales and all post1931 entrants, provided that such a Government servant may elect to continue to draw pay in the existing scale until the date on which he earns his next or subsequent increment in the existing scale or until he vacates his post or ceases to draw pay in that time scale of pay.

Explanation. 1.—The option to retain the existing scale under clause (b) may also be exercised by a pre-1931 entrant drawing pay in a prescribed scale on the 1st July, 1959 in accordance with the provisions of rule 4 of the Central Civil Services (Revision of Pay) Rules, 1947.

Explanation 12.—The option to retain the existing scale under clause (b) shall be admissible only in respect of one existing scale.

Explanation 3.—Where . a Government servant exercises the option under clause (b) to retain, the existing scale in respect of a post held by him in an officiating capacity, his substantive pay for the purpose of Fundamental Rule 31 shall be the substantive pay which he would have drawn had he retained the existing scale .in respect of the permanent post on which he holds a lien or would have held a lien had his lien not been suspended.

9. Exercise of option.—(1) The option under rule 8(.a) or 8(b) shall be exercised in writing so as to roach the authority mentioned in sub-rule (2) within four months of the date of issue o" these rules or of the orders revising the existing scale, whichever is later provided that;—

(i) in the case of a Government seivant who is on that date out ol India on leave or deputation or foreign service or active service, the said option shall be exercised in writing so as to reach the prescribed authority within four months of the date of his taking over charge of his post in India; and

(ji) wheie a Government servant is under suspension on the 1st July, 19,59, the option may be exercised within four months of the date of his return to duty if that date is later than the date prescribed in this sub-rule.

(2) The option shall be intimated by the Government servant in the appropriate form appended to these rules:—

(a) if ht is a gazetted Government servant, to his Accounts Officer;

(b) if he is a non-gazetted Government servant, to the Head of his Office.

(3) If the declaration regarding option is not received within the time mentioned in sub-rule (1),

(i) a pre-1931 entrant who has the option to elect the revised scales of pay under rule 8(a) shall be deemed not to have elected the revised scales of pay; and 4 3 Q / J THK GA/ETTK OF INDIA EXTRAORDINARY

(ii) any other Government servant shall be deemed to have elected the revised scales of pay with effect from the 1st July, 1959.

(4) The option once exercised is final.

10. Fixation of Initial pay In the revised scales,—(1) Subject to the provisions of sub-rule (2), the initial pay of a Government servant who elects or is deemed to have elected under rule 9(3) the revised scale, from the 1st July, 1959 shall, unless in any case the President by special order otherwise directs, be fixed separately in respect of his substantive pay in the permanent post on which he hoi is n hen or would have held a lien if it had not been suspended, and in respect of hi? pay in the officiating post held by him, in the following manner:—

(i) Where a single existing scale has been replaced by a single revised scale, the pay in the revised scale shall be fixed at the stage arrived at after adding the same number of stages to the minimum of the revised scale ai were necessary to arrive at the basic pay in the existing scale from its minimum.

(ii) Where two existing scales in the same line of promotion have been replaced by a single revised scale, the pay in the revised scale in respect of persons who are in the lower of the two existing scales shall be fixed as in clause (i) and in respect of person? in the higher of the two existing scales, at the stage in the revised scale arrived at by deducting the same number of stages from the maximum of the; reyised scale as were necessary to arrive at the basic pay in the existing scale from its maximum.

(liii Where two existing scales not in the same line Qf promotion, or more than two existing scales, whether in the same line of promotion or not, have been replaced by a single revised scale, the pay in the revised scale shall be fixed as in clause d) except that for such fixation the minimum of the revised scale shaJl be deemed to be the stage where the emoluments in the revised scale are equal to the emoluments at the minimum of the relevant existing scale or if there is no such stage, the stage next above.

NoTt 1.—Where a Government servant is holdinff a permanent post and is officiating in a higher posL and the scales applicable to these two posts are merged into one scale, the pay shall be fixed under this sub-rule with reference to the officiating post only, provided he has continuously officiated in that post for not less than one year, and the pay so fixed shall be treated as substantive pay. Where such a Government servant has not completed one year's continuous service in the higher officiating post on the 1st July, 1959 his pay in the revised scale shall be fixed separately with reference to his substantive pay and officiating pay in the existing scales and his pay in the revised scale fixed with reference to the officiating pay shall be treated as substantive pay in that scaJc after rendering service for the period by which it fell short of one year on the 1st July, 1959 provided ii is certified by the appointing authority that he would have continued to officiate in the higher officiating post during this period had the revised scale not been introduced. If, however, the appointing authority certifies that he would have reverted to the lower post during this period, his pay in the revised scale would from the date on which he would have reverted, be regulated on the basis of the pay fixed on the 1st July, l&5t> with reference to his substantive pay in the luwer post.

The provisions of this note shall apply, pnutatis mutandis to Gove nment servants holding in an officiating capacity posts on different existing scales which have been replaced by a single revised scale.

NOTE 2.—Where a single existing scale has been replaced by a single revised, scale or two or more existing scales not in the sarne line of promotion are replaced by a single scale, a Government servant who has served on the maximum of the existing scale shall be given increments as under on the revised scale after his pay has been fixed under rule 10(1)(i) or 10(1)(iii) provided that such extra increments do not result in his exceeding the appropriate ceiling limit referred to in rule 10(2).

(a) Where the service on the maximum h one year or more One but less than three yeari. increment

(b) Where the service on the maximum is three years or Two more i n ( r t merits S E C . 3 ( | . G J ~* ~,

(2) The revised emoluments shall not exceed the present emoluments by more lhan:—

(1) Rs. 5 or Rs, 7-50 nP. in the case of revised scales ending at Rs. 100 or less according as the Government servant concerned has rendered service not exceeding ten years or exceeding ten years respectively in the same existing scale;

(ii) Rs. 10 in the case of revised scales ending at Rs. 200 or less, but above Rs. 100;

(iii) Rs. 20 in the case of revised scales ending at Rs. 300 or less, but above Rs. 200;

(iv) Rs. 30 in the case of revised scales ending at Rs. 600 or less, but above Rs 300;

(v) Rs. 40 in the case of revised scales ending at Rs. 1,000 or less, but above Rs. 600;

(,vi) Rs. 50 in the case of revised scales ending above Rs. 1,000. Provided that— '(a) in the case of a Government servant whose present emoluments are not less than Rs. 1,100 per mensem, the revised emoluments shall not exceed the present emoluments;

(b) if the present emoluments exceed the revised emoluments in the case of any Government servant the difference shall be allowed as personal pay to be absorbed in future increases in pay.

NOTE 1.-—Where the revised emoluments at the stage arrived at under Kubrule (1) exceed the present emoluments by more than the ceiling limits prescribed in sub-rule (2) initial pay shall be fixed at the highest stage in the revised scale at which the revised emoluments payable do not exceed the present emoluments by more than the ceiling limits, and the difference between the present emoluments plus the ceiling, and the revised emoluments shall be allowed as personal pay to be absorbed in future increases in pay.

NOTE 2.—The ceiling limits prescribed in this sub-rule do not apply in cases where the initial pay is fixed at the minimum of revised scale.

(3) Subject to the provisions of'rule 8, if the pay as fixed in the officiating post under sub-rules (1) and (2) is equal to or lower than the pay as fixed in a substantive- post, the former shall be re-fixed at the stage next above the substantive pay.

11. Date ol next increment In the revised scales.—The next increment of a Government servant whose pay has been fixed in the revised scale in accordance with rule 10(1) shall be granted on the date he would have drawn his increment, had he continued in the existing scale provided that:

(i) where the revised pay is fixed at the minimum of the time scale and on such fixation the revised emoluments exceed the present emoluments by more than the appropriate ceiling limit in terms of note 2 to rule 10(2), the next increment shall be granted on the date it falls due in the revised scale; and fiii the next increment shall be granted on the 2nd July, 1959 to a Government servant whose pay fixed on the 1st July, 1959 in the revised scale is at the same stage as the one fixed for another Government servant drawing pay at a lower stage than his in the same existing scale.

Explanation.—For the purposes of rules 10 and 11 increment shall be construed to mean stages in the time scale and not actual increases in pay, Where an increment on an existing or revised scale is not annual, the increment shall be construed as carrying as many stages as the number of years of service required to be rendered for earning an increment.

12. Special provision for regulating pay of Class IV employees below the age of 18 years.—Notwithstanding the provisions of these rules, the pay of a Government servant below the age of 18 years holding a post in the revised scale of Rs. 70-1-80-EB-1-85, shall be Rs 70 reduced by two rupees for each year by which his age falls below 18 years and shall be increased annually by two rupees from nia next birthday onwards until he attains the age of 18 years when he will draw the minimum of the time scale. ^ CA - OF , A A( I sARY

13. Fixation of pay in the revised seaioi subsequent to the 1st July 1959.—(1) Where a Government servant continues to draw his pay in the existing scale and is brought over to a revised stale from a date later than the 1st July, 1959, his pay in the revised scale shall be fixed under the Fundamental Kules and fop this purpose his pay in the existing scale shall be deemed to be pay plus dearness pay and dearness allowance, provided that the pay so fixed shall not exceed the pay that would have been admissible on the date of being brought over to the revised scale had he elected the revised scales with effect from the 1st July, 1959.

(2) A Government servant who has officiated in a post prior to the 1st day of July, 1959 but was not holding that post on that date and who on subsequent appointment to that post draws pay in the revised scale of pay shall be allowed the benefit of the proviso to the Fundamental Rule 22 to the extent it would havi? been admissible to him had he been holding that post on the 1st July, 1959 and elected the revised scale of pay on that date. 14, Overriding effect of the rules.—In cases where the pay is regulated under these rules, the provisions of the Fundamental Rules, the Revised Rates of Pay Rules, 1933, and the Central Civil Services (Revision of Pay) Rules, 1947, shall not apply to the extent they are inconsistent with these rules, FORMS OF OPTION rSee rule 9(2)] FORM I For pre-1931 entrants who had not elected the prescribed scales of pay * (i) I, , hereby elect the revised scales with effect from the 1st July, 1959. *(ii) I, , hereby elect to retain the pre-1931 scales. *dil) I , hereby elect to retain the existing scale of pay of my substantive/officiating post mentioned below until *the date of my next Increment the dale of my subsequent Increment raising my pay to Rs I vacate or cease to draw pay In the existing scale and to retain the pre-1931 scales in respect of the remaining posts.

Rule 1.—This rule "is self-explanatory.

Rule 2.-—This rule lays down "the categories of employees to whorjti the rules apply.

Except-for the categories excluded under clause (ii), the ryles are applicable toall persons under the rulc-maklhg control of the President serving in Departments paid from Civil Estimates. They do not apply to the employees of the Railway Department and Civilian personnel paid from Defence Services Estimates, for whom separate rules will be issued by the departments concerned. The rules, however, apply to work-charged establishment.

Rule 'S.—This rule follows the rule promulgated in the Ministry of Home Affairs Notification No. 10H/54-Ests (A), dated the 20th November,

1954. This is an enabling provision to make relaxations In exceptional cases where the operation of the rules may cause hardship.

Rule 4.—This rule is self-explanatory.

Rule 5: (1) 'Buslc Pay'.—This term has been defined to mean pay as denned in Fundamental Rule 9(21) (a) (i).

The intention is that the pay in the revised scales should be tixed only witty reference to basic pay of the individual and other emolumenis classed as 'Pay' should not be taken into account for this purpose.

Special pay attached to an existing scale of pay in lieu of a separate tcale of pay and merged in the revised scale will be ticated as a part oi 'basic pay' in the existing scale.

If personal pay drawn in the existing scale does not exceed the difference between the revised emoluments and present emoluments, no personal pay will be adnvjijible in addition to pay in the revised scale. If, however, it exceeds the difference, the amount by which it exceeds the difference will be allowed as personal pay to be absorbed in future increases in pay in the revised scale,

(2) 'Emoluments'.—The term 'dearness allowance' in this definition includes 'interim relief of Rs. 5/- allowed with effect from the 1st July, 1957.

(3) 'Existing scale'.—-This term has been defined to indicate not only the scale of the post actually held by a Government servant on the 1st July, 1959 but also the scales of other posts which a Government servant would have held but for his being on deputation out of India or on leave or on foreign service or officiating in other posts. The intention is that the pay of a Government servant should be fixed separately in the revised scales in respect of existing scales of pay applicable to his substantive post and the officiating post(s) held by him or which would have been held by him on the 1st July, 1959.

Scales which ait_ personal to certain incumbents will also be tieated as: 'existing scale' for the purposes of these rules.

(4) 'IPost-1931 entrant'.—The definition of this term is self-explanatory.

(5) 'Pre-1931 entrant'.—This follows the definition of the term contained in the Central Civil Services (Revision of Pay) Rules, 1947.

(6) 'Pre-1931 scale'.— ~| Y The definitions of these terms are self-explanatoiy.

(7) 'Prescribed seal*- j

(8) 'Present emoluments'.—In the definition of this term also the term 'dearness allowance' includes the 'interim relief of Rs. 5/- granted with effect from the 1st July. 1957.

(9) 'Revised emoluments'.—This term' means pay as fixed in the revised scale plus dearness allowance at the revised rates which is Rs. 10/- for pay below Bs. 150/- p.m. and Rs. 20/ for pay of Rs. 150/- p.m. and above but not exceeding Es. 300/- p.m. with marginal adjustment up to Rs. 320/-. Under the revised pay structure dearness allqwance Is not admissible on a pay of or exceeding Rs. 320/p.m.

Sea. I (11)] 439/l?3

(10) Hevised scale'.—This expression should hereafter be used exclusively for referring to the new scales introduced on the recommendations of the Payi Com> mission. The scales introduced under the Revised Rates of Pay Rules, 1933 and connected orders should be referred to as the revised scales (1933).

(11) 'Schedule'.—The definition is self-explanatory.

Rule 6.—This rule is self-explanatory.

Rules 7 & 8.—The intention is that all Government servants should be brought over IO the revised scales except: —

(1) A pre-1931 entrant who has not elected the prescribed scales and who (a) does not elect the revised scales but continues on pre-1931 scales of pay where there is a pre-1931 scale for the post he is holding; and (b) who is not entitled to elect the revised scales. The option to elect revised scales will not be admissible to such a pie-1931 entrant unless the prescribed scales of all the posts held by him in substantive as well as in officiating capacity undergo revision under these rules. For this purpose, prescription of new scales by the merger of the dearness allowance shall be construed as 'revision' under these rules.

(2) Pre-1931 entrants who had elected the prescribed scales but who elect ta draw pay in the existing scale; and

(3) All post-1931 entrants who elect to draw pay in the existing scales.

Those who exercise the option to continue on the existing scale of pay (whether it is a pre-1931 scale or a prescribed scale) will continue to draw the dearness pay and dearness allowance at the rates in force on the 30th June, 1959 and the dearnesa pay will count towards travelling allowance, house rent and compensatory .allowances, emoluments for pension etc., to the extent it so counted on the said date.

If a Government servant is holding a permanent post in a substantive capacity and officiating in a higher post or would have officiated in one or more posts but for his being on deputation etc., he has the option to retain the existing scale only in respect of one scale (save that a pre-1931 entrant who had not opted for the prescribed scale and who doec- not now opt for the revised scale, will have the right to remain on the pre-1931 SCBICS of pay for all posts held by him). Such a Government servant may retain the existing scale applicable to his permanent post or to any one of the officiating posts. In respect of the remaining posts he will necessarily have to be brought over to the rcvi-ed scales,

Explanation 3 to Rule 8.—As a Government servant will have the option to retain the existing scale in respect of only one 'existing scale', a Government servant who retains the existing scale in respect of his officiating post, will be brought over to the revised scale in respect of his permanent post. In the revised scale of the permanent post bulk of dearness allowance stands merged whereas la the officiating scale he will be allowed the existing rates of dearness pay and dearness allowance.

If his pay were fixed in the higher officiating post with reference to the pay on the revised scale in the substantive post it will give him a double advantage, This explanation is designed to avoid the benefit of double dearness allowance in such cases.

Rule 9.—This rule prescribes the manner In which option has to be exercised Hnd also the authority who should be appraised of such option. The option has to be exerciser! in the appropriate form appended to the rules. It should be noted that it is not sufficient for a Government servant to exercise the option within the specified time limit but also to ensure that It reaches the prescribed authority within the time limit, Tn the case of persons who are outside India at the time lhere rules are promulgated, the period within which the option has to be exercised is four months from the date they take over charge in India. In the rase of Government servants the revised scales of whose posts are announced HUb"eqnonl to the date of issue r>f those rules, the period of four months will run from the date of such announcement.

Sub-rule (3) indicates the eligibility to draw pay in the revised scales or the pre-1931 scales in cases where a Government servant does not exercise any option.

A pre-1931 entrant who had not elected the p-escrlbed scales of pay and Is drawing pay in a prescribed scale of a post under rule 4 of the Central Civil Services (Revision of Pay) Rules, 1947, will be brought over to the. revised scale only in respect of that particular post. 439/174 T H E G A Z E T T E O F INDIA EXTRAORDINARY

[PAIT II The phrase 'active service' used in this rule refers to service abroad with the* Armed Forces and does not include service in the Indian missions abroad.

Rule 10(1).—This rule deals with the actual fixation of pay in the revised aeales on 1st July, 1959.

Clause (i).—Clause (i) deals with cases where a single existing scale is replaced by a single revised scale. The number of stages (where the incremental rate Is biennial each increment is construed to have two stages and where it is triennial each Increment is construed to have three stages and so on) that are necessary tcx roach the basic pay from the minimum of the existing scale as on the 1st July, 1959 will be calculated first. The same number of stages will be added to the minimum of the revised scale and the stage so arrived at will be the pay in the revised scale unless the additional emoluments payable at the stage exceed the (tailings prescribed under rule 10(2) in which case the pay will be fixed asprovided in note 1 to rule 10(2). Two examples of pay fixation under this Clause are given below:— , Example I.

Details of the case :—

(0 Existing scale Rs. 200—15—380—E.B.—20—500.

(if) Revised scale Rs. 270—15—435—E.B.—20—575. ' in the exi'ting scale on 1-7-1959 Rs. 400/-.

Dearness pay and allowance (including interim relief). . . . .

Rs, 70/-.

Total present emoluments . . . .

Rs. 470'-.

The present basic pay of Rs. 400/- in the existing scale is arrived at byaiding 13 stages (12 stages of Rs. 15/- each and one stage of Rs. 20/-) to theminimum of the existing scale.

The pay In the revised scale will therefore befixed at a stage arrived at after adding 13 stages (11 stages of Rs. 15/- each and 2 stages of Rs. 20/- each) to the minimum of the revised scale, which will come to Rs. 475/-.

This exceeds the; present emoluments of Rs. 470/- by Rs. 5/- whichaddition is within the prescribed ceiling. Hence his basic pay in the revised floale will be fixed at the stage of Rs. 475/-.

The present basic pay (RE. 230) hi the existing scale is arrived at by adding 1-2 stages (3 stages from Rs. 130 to Rs. 140, 2 stages from Rs. 140 to Rs. 160, and 7 stages from Rs. 160 to Rs. 230) to the minimum of that scale. The cay in the revised scale will be fixed at a stage arrived at by adding 12 stages _(8 of Rs. 10 each and 4 of Rs. 15 each) to the minimum (Rs. 210) of the revised scale which will come to Rs. 350/- p.m. This exceeds the present emoluments of Rs. 300/- by Rs. 50/-. But the total emoluments in the revised scale cannot exceed Rs. 330/i.e. the present emoluments of Rs. 300/- and the ceiling of Rs. 30/-. As there Is no stage of Rs. 330/- in the revised scale, the pay in that scale will be fixed at the next below stage of Rs. 320/- and personal pay of Rs. 10/- will be allowed which will be absorbed in subsequent increases in pay.

Clause (U).—This appjies to cases where two scales in the same line of promotion are merged into one revised scale. The pay of those who are in the lower grade in the existing scale will be fixed in the manner indicated against oiaus* (1). In respect of those who are In the higher grade in the existing scale tke stages which are necessary; to reach the maximum of the existing scale from MM basic pay as on 1st July 1959 will be calculated first. The same number of itages will be deducted from the maximum of the revised scale and the stage 479/* 75: so arrived at will be the pay in the revised scale, subject to the ceilings not being exceeded. An example of fixation of pay under this clause Is Riven below:—

The pay in the revised' scale will be fixed at a stage arrived at by adding 5 stages of Rs. 25/- each to the minimum (Rs. 350/-) of the revised scale which will come to Rs. 475/-. This ^exceeds the present emoluments of Rs. 470/- by Rs, 5/-. This additional gain Is covered under the prescribed ceiling.

The revised pay will, therefore, be fixed at Rs. 475/-.

In the second case of 'B' the present basic pay of Rs. 650/- in the existing; scale is arrived at by deducting 5 stages of Rs. 30/- each from the maximum of the existing scale. Clause (111).—This clause relates to the fixation of pav, where two existing scales not in the same line of promotion or more than two existing scales, whether In the same line of promotion or not, are replaced by a single revised scale. The cases that would be covered by this clause are workshop staff, clerical supervisory staff, staff icar drivers and other categories of staff which the Commission have grouped together and for which they have recommended a common revised scale.

The method of fixation of pay will be the same as in clause (i), but would be with reference to a notional minimum which should be taken as the stage in the revised scale which corresponds to the emoluments at the minimum of the existing scale less the dearness allowance admissible at the revised rates at that stage, or If there Is no such stage, the stage next above. Us. 175/The present basic pay (Rs. 144/-) of 'A! in the existing scale at (i) (a) above is .arrived, at by adding 7 stages (4 of Rs. 5/- each and 3 of. Rs. 8/- each) to the minimum of that scale.

The minimum (Rs. 100/-) of the existing scale at (i) (a) above plus clearness allowance thereon is equal to Rs. 155/-. For fixation of pay in £he revised scale the stage of Rs. 145/- in that scale will be treated as its notional minimum as the emoluments (Rs. 145+10 d.a. at revised rate) at this stage are equal to Rs. 155/i.e. the minimum (Rs. 100/-) of the scale at (1) (a) above and dearness allowance admissible thereon at existing rate. A's pay will, therefore, be fixed In the revised scale by adding 7 stages (3 of Rs. 5/- each and 4 of Rs. 8/- each) to Rs. 148^which will come to Rs. 192/-. This is a stage in the revised scale. In addition he will get d.a. of Rs. 20/- at the revised rate. His total emoluments In the revised scale will, therefore, be Rs. 212/- (Rs. 192+20) which exceed the present emoluments of Rs. 204/- by Rs.. 8/-. This additional gain L= within the prescribed ceiling.

The present basic pay (Rs. 200/-) of 'B' in the existing scale at (i) (b) abovi is arrived at p&' adding 18 stages (8 of Rs. 5/- each and 10 of Rs. 8/- each) to the minimum of' that scale.

The emoluments admissible at the minimum of thi3 existing scale are Rs. 136/- (i.e. Rs. 80/-+Rs. 55/- d.a, thereon). In the revised scale there is no stage (i.e. of Rs. 125) where the emoluments admissible will be equal to Rs. 135/- and in any case the minimum of the revised scale cannot be reduced. Therefore, the pay of 'B' will be fixed in the revised scale by adding 18 stages (4 of Rs. 5/- each and 14 of Rs, 8/- each) to the minimum of that scale (Rs. 140/-) which will come to Rs. 272/-. In addition h-e will get d.a. Rs. 20/at the revised rate.

His total emoluments (Rs. 272 + 20 i.e. R's. 202) in thff revised scale scale are more than his present emolument? of Rs. 265/- by Rs. 27/-;

This exceeds the ceiling limit which is Rs. 20/- in this case. Since the gain is more than what is permitted B's total emoluments in the revised scale cannot exceed Rs. 285/- i.e. his present emoluments of Rs. 265/- and the ceiling of Rs. 20/-, Deducting from Rs. 285/- dearness allowance -of Rs. 20/- at^the revised rates, B's pay should be fixed at Rs. 265/- but since there is no stage ef Rs. 26&/in the revised scale his pay in that .calo will be fixed at the next below stage of Rs. 264/- and he will be allowed a personal pay of Rs. 1/- to be absorbed in subsequent increases in pay.

The present basic pay (Rs. 115/-) of 'C in the existing scale at (i) (c) above is arrived at by adding 3 stages of Rs. 5/- each to the minimum of that scale. The emoluments admissible at the minimum of existing scale are equal to Rs. 155/(i.e. Rs. 100/- plus Rs. 55/- d.a.). For fixation of pay in the revised tcale the stage of Rs. 145/- in that scale will be treated as its notional minimum as the emoluments (viz. Rs. 145 plus d.a. of Rs. 10/- at revised rate) admissible at this stage are equal to Rs. 155/- (i.e. the minimum of the existing scale plus d.a. thereon). His pay will then be fixed by adding 3 stages of Rs. 5/- each to Rs. 145/- which will come to Rs. 160/-. In addition he will get d.a. of Rs. 20/- at the revised rate. His total emoluments in the revised scale will be Rs. 180/- (Rs, 160+20) which exceed tho present emoluments ofRs. 175/- by Rs. 3/-. This additional gain is within the • prescribed celling.

Note 1 to Rule 10 (1).—This note Is self-explanatory.

Note 2 to Rule 10 (1).—THis provides for weightage for service at the maximum of the existing scale. This is, powever, admissible if one single scale is replaced by a single revised scale or two or more existing scales not in the same line of promotion are replaced by a single revised scale. It Is not admissible whan two or more scales In the same line of promotion are replaced by a single scale. This benefit is, however, subject to the tellings and also to the condition that by the
